Andrews , engineer revenue agent , Bureau of Internal Revenue , Treasury Depart￾ment , who , during the month of September , 1939 , will reach the retirement age pre￾scribed for automatic separation from the service , applicable to him, be exempted
from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year :
NOW, THEREFORE , by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 , 47 Stat. 382 , 404 (U.

### T05d04a3ca5dd27489f947611

Text hash d3751938b6785fe2323900f23e77e0b405af3a1e3eadfe879db98be8b3db9255; absolute characters 166–462; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-app-executive-orders-8207-3a5a242a22.txt

```text
Bowerman, Librarian, Free Public Library of the District of Columbia, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939, by Executive Order No. 7892 of May 18, 1938, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:
```

### T079f163c3cb986ea93880b60

Text hash c1c34841befe72da293e156ddab74c19d648af2abe60c68459ebbca8bb5887ce; absolute characters 447–735; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-app-executive-orders-8218-9f7771ac0b.txt

```text
Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Herbert Vansant from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending August 31, 1940.
```

### T0c0aac58a4e4e0741b3cf437

Text hash f847ce7b75098ce9f55d95481119dd54aa471682086e8836633ca87dd609c442; absolute characters 0–1314; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7413-exemption-edward-m-weeks-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *";

And Whereas the public interest requires that Edward M. Weeks, letter engraver, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury Department, who, during the month of August, 1936, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until September 1, 1937;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Edward M. Weeks from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until September 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

July 10, 1936.

```

### T0ce5c0eba822e5033a7596ec

### T13a213f9e03867a4d025cb76

Text hash 1ec11488e5f1d729851b4f254a051911011f964586eff264b7761e689b082808; absolute characters 0–743; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7945-exemption-edward-m-weeks-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Edward M. Weeks, letter engraver, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury Department, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for periods totaling two years by Executive Orders No. 7413 of July 10, 1936, and No. 7635 of June 16, 1937, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Edward M. Weeks from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending August 31, 1939.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

August 4, 1938.

```

### T1763b4f6b77c9a927ae14a07

Text hash b73185b54eae3df7bcc1853b0451f792b209e69ae7702167e00538309943dfe3; absolute characters 0–699; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8286-exemption-hugh-j-murray-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Hugh J. Murray, master mechanic, Torpedo Tube Shop, Navy Yard, Washington, D.C., who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Hugh J. Murray from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending November 30, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

November 16, 1939.

```

```text
Executive Order
SEP 5-1939
HARVARD LAW LIBRARY
EXEMPTION OF HERBERT VANSANT FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FOR AGE
WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Herbert Vansant ,
senior civil engineer , Engineer Department at Large, War Department , who , during
the month of August , 1939 , will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service , applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retire￾ment for age for a period of one year:
NOW , THEREFORE , by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 , 47 Stat . 382 , 404 (U.
```

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Ales Hrdlicka, Curator of Physical Anthropology in the United States National Museum, Smithsonian Institution, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for periods totaling two years by Executive Orders No. 8058 of February 23, 1939, and No. 8368 of March 7, 1940, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Ales Hrdlicka from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending March 31, 1942.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

March 12, 1941.

```

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Louis A. Simon, Supervising Architect of the Public Buildings Administration, Federal Works Agency, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for periods totaling four years by Executive Orders No. 7581 of March 19, 1937, No. 7824 of February 25, 1938, No. 8046 of February 11, 1939, and No. 8369 of March 7, 1940, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of six months:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Louis A. Simon from compulsory retirement for age for a period of six months ending November 30, 1941.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

April 28, 1941.

```

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that William M. Smith, Special Assistant to the Chief of the Bureau of Yards and Docks, Navy Department, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1940, by Executive Order No. 8209 of July 17, 1939, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

July 18, 1940.

```

### T89b6ca1736b40b3cbb066f59

Text hash 3eefba4322139729618ba7274bf3e3a4bc41d4567df532a5d19e7392c1e29075; absolute characters 0–1315; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7635-exemption-edward-m-weeks-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * * ";

And Whereas the public interest requires that Edward M. Weeks, letter engraver, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury Department, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7413 of July 10, 1936, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until September 1, 1938;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Edward M. Weeks from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until September 1, 1938.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

June 16, 1937.

```

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

February 23, 1939.

```

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Edward A. Neill, Chief Mechanic, Naval Hospital, Washington, D.C., who, during the month of December, 1939, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

October 26, 1939.

```

Text hash c254ef5404384d8469fa5a7c680836dfd65d488b7f5d146c29dc66a8dcd3d6f9; absolute characters 155–504; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-app-executive-orders-8231-96bde16499.txt

```text
Weeks, letter engraver, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury Department, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for periods totaling three years by Executive Orders No. 7413 of July 10, 1936, No. 7635 of June 16, 1937, and No. 7945 of August 4, 1938, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of six months.
```

### Teee76fd237d33958dfe8bab9

Text hash 6f6c07354299b68564d937be80b61d42daaf22dbbf5778595dc7e84732db41f9; absolute characters 0–725; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8228-exemption-william-t-andrews-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that William T. Andrews, engineer revenue agent, Bureau of Internal Revenue, Treasury Department, who, during the month of September, 1939, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said William T. Andrews from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

August 24, 1939.

```

Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (common-crawl capture) has the heading \"Executive Order 8204—Exemption of Michael E. Gorman From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", which names this order number, its date (1939-07-14) agrees with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. I read the whole text: one recital and one operative sentence, so the clause inventory is the single whole-paragraph clause. The sentence \"hereby exempt the said Michael E. Gorman from compulsory retirement for age for a period of six months ending February 29, 1940\" fixes the end of the exemption on February 29, 1940; that is the first end, with the exclusive reading disclosed. The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned no hit. The packet's name search of held order texts for the person named in this order is also cited. Other leads and later events are discussed in the clause reason. No held instrument revokes, supersedes or terminates this exemption before its stated date. No agency or Civil Service Commission record of the employee's separation or death is held, so no earlier end is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order directs nothing besides the exemption: no report, appointment, transfer or other independent command appears in its text.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption let Michael E. Gorman stay in service beyond the retirement age; service rendered while it was in force is an accrued effect that the expiry does not undo. The order contains no continuing command of its own after February 29, 1940; the statute it cites (section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404) is its authority only.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order is a one-person order about Michael E. Roosevelt), a one-person exemption from compulsory retirement for age. Text and identity: the held text is the American Presidency Project transcription (grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (common-crawl capture) has the heading \"Executive Order 8228—Exemption of William T. Andrews From Compulsory Retirement For Age\", which names this order number, its date (1939-08-24) agrees with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. I read the whole text: one recital and one operative sentence, so the clause inventory is the single whole-paragraph clause. The sentence \"hereby exempt the said William T. Andrews from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1940\" fixes the end of the exemption on September 30, 1940; that is the first end, with the exclusive reading disclosed. The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned one hit, an \"Amended by\" note naming the later further-exemption order EO 8554. The packet's name search of held order texts for the person named in this order is also cited. Other leads and later events are discussed in the clause reason. No held source shows an earlier end (revocation, supersession, or an earlier separation or death of the employee), so none is claimed.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier end than September 30, 1940 tested: The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned one hit, an \\\"Amended by\\\" note naming the later further-exemption order EO 8554. No held instrument revokes, supersedes or terminates this exemption before its stated date. No agency or Civil Service Commission record of the employee's separation or death is held, so no earlier end is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order directs nothing besides the exemption: no report, appointment, transfer or other independent command appears in its text.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption let William T. Andrews stay in service beyond the retirement age; service rendered while it was in force is an accrued effect that the expiry does not undo. The order contains no continuing command of its own after September 30, 1940; the statute it cites (section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404) is its authority only.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order is a one-person order about William T. Andrews; its identity rests on the number, date and subject in the APP page heading and the held text. Its whole text is one recital and one operative sentence, so one whole-paragraph clause is the complete inventory, and the stated period \\\"one year ending September 30, 1940\\\" is the only duration in Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (common-crawl capture) has the heading \"Executive Order 8268—Exemption of George W. Stose from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1940\" fixes the end of the exemption on October 31, 1940; that is the first end, with the exclusive reading disclosed. The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned no hit. Murray stay in service beyond the retirement age; service rendered while it was in force is an accrued effect that the expiry does not undo. The order contains no continuing command of its own after November 30, 1940; the statute it cites (section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404) is its authority only.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order is a one-person order about Hugh J. Murray; its identity rests on the number, date and subject in the APP page heading and the held text. Its whole text is one recital and one operative sentence, so one whole-paragraph clause is the complete inventory, and the stated period \\\"one year ending November 30, 1940\\\" is the only duration in the APP page record with the same sha256 names order number 8368, the date 1940-03-07 and the subject, and its text hash is the held text's hash. The order is a short whole document: a recital paragraph, one operative paragraph and the signature and date line. Inventory: the one operative paragraph is the only clause; the recital is the President's finding that the public interest requires the exemption and commands nothing. End: the order exempts Ales Hrdlicka from compulsory retirement for age for a term it states as 'for a period of one year ending March 31, 1941', so it ends by its own terms on 1941-03-31 (inclusive reading; the exclusive reading ends the term when that day begins). Later instruments: the packet's later-events search of Office of the Federal Register disposition notes (1937 onward), its person-name search of held order texts, and its current-law searches of the U.S. Code and the eCFR were selected after reading their coverage notes. The later-events search lists two hits for this order. The later-events lead file records both as Office of the Federal Register cross-reference pointers, one to the earlier exemption order for the same person (Executive Order 8058 of February 23, 1939) and one to the further exemption order (Executive Order 8710 of March 12, 1941); neither is a revocation or supersession. Bishop From Compulsory Retirement for Age; May 18, 1940; grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved). I read all 718 characters. APP page check against the mirror record (Common Crawl capture): its heading names Executive Order 8413, its date 1940-05-18 agrees with the signing line of the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. The held text prints the end year as 1041, an evident misprint: the order is dated May 18, 1940 and an exemption ending in 1041 is impossible. The operative sentence says the exemption runs for one year, the recital says the employee reaches retirement age in June 1940, and the later EO 8810 of June 30, 1941 recites a one-year exemption by this order and further exempts him for a year ending June 30, 1942, so the intended end is June 30, 1941. Whole-order status is no (provisional, already adopted; not changed here). The order exempts Hubert K. Bishop from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending June 30 of the year printed 1041 and read as 1941, and ends on that date. The end is the order's own fixed term, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. Held later events: EO 8810, a further exemption for a later period, recorded as a later event and not as an end. Savings: accrued effects of service during the period. The later orders named are not ends of this order.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is shown. Machine searches read for coverage: current-law:usc (U.S. Code release point 119-121, current through 2026-10-09; citations of this order by number; 0 hits), current-law:ecfr (eCFR point-in-time 2026-10-14 and GovInfo CFR bulk XML; citations of this order by number; 0 hits), later-events:ofr_catalog (Federal Register executive-order catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of saved order texts; 1 hits), namier:held_texts_person_name (the employee's full name in other held order texts; 0 hits).
